This is definitely the first time I've experienced anything like this, and it's been really overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you've had two years or more where you felt down or depressed, even if it wasn't as severe as what you're experiencing now?\n\nPatient: No, I definitely haven't felt like this for two years. This all started just about five months ago, and before that, I was doing completely fine.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how this lack of motivation and down mood have affected your daily life, such as your work, relationships, or ability to take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: At work, I've completely lost the drive to start or finish even basic tasks, which is making it really hard to keep up. I've also started pulling away from my friends and family, and I just don't have the energy to put any effort into maintaining those relationships anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience feelings of guilt, worthlessness, or excessive self-blame, and if so, how often do you have these feelings?\n\nPatient: Yes, I constantly feel worthless and useless, and I've been burdened by this intense guilt almost every day for the last five months. I find myself obsessively replaying minor mistakes from my past and constantly blaming myself for trivial daily events. It feels like a non-stop loop in my head that I just can't escape.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about these obsessive thoughts of guilt and self-blame, such as how they started, how long they last, and if you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to try to alleviate them?"
  }

They started about five months ago when this depression set in, and I constantly replay minor past mistakes, feeling like I deserve to be punished. I don't perform any physical rituals or behaviors to try to stop them; instead, I just get stuck in a loop of blaming myself in my mind.

It's gotten to the point where I struggle to do anything at all, which is why I finally decided to seek help today.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if this feeling of being down and unmotivated has been consistent over the past five months, or have you had any periods where you felt somewhat better?\n\nPatient: It has been completely constant, without any breaks or better days at all. For the entire five months, I've felt this heavy lack of motivation and down mood every single day.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any episodes of depression like this before, or is this the first time you've felt this way?\n\nPatient: No, I've never felt this way before. This is definitely the first time I've experienced anything like this, and it's been really overwhelming.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me if you've had two years or more where you felt down or depressed, even if it wasn't as severe as what you're experiencing now?\n\nPatient: No, I definitely haven't felt like this for two years. This all started just about five months ago, and before that, I was doing completely fine.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how this lack of motivation and down mood have affected your daily life, such as your work, relationships, or ability to take care of yourself?\n\nPatient: At work, I've completely lost the drive to start or finish even basic tasks, which is making it really hard to keep up. I've also started pulling away from my friends and family, and I just don't have the energy to put any effort into maintaining those relationships anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience feelings of guilt, worthlessness, or excessive self-blame, and if so, how often do you have these feelings?\n\nPatient: Yes, I constantly feel worthless and useless, and I've been burdened by this intense guilt almost every day for the last five months. I find myself obsessively replaying minor mistakes from my past and constantly blaming myself for trivial daily events. It feels like a non-stop loop in my head that I just can't escape.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about these obsessive thoughts of guilt and self-blame, such as how they started, how long they last, and if you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to try to alleviate them?\n\nPatient: They started about five months ago when this depression set in, and I constantly replay minor past mistakes, feeling like I deserve to be punished. I don't perform any physical rituals or behaviors to try to stop them; instead, I just get stuck in a loop of blaming myself in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the obsessive thoughts of guilt and self-blame are a primary part of your distress, or do you feel they are more a consequence of your overall feeling of being down and unmotivated?"
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Honestly, I'm not sure which one is the cause and which is the effect. The constant self-blame and my lack of motivation feel so deeply interconnected and equally overwhelming that it's hard to separate them.

Loss of Interest: Reduced interest in previously enjoyed hobbies or leisure activities.\n  - Social Withdrawal: Reduced interest or effort in social interactions and maintaining relationships.\n  - Loss of Motivation: Lack of motivation to initiate or complete work or tasks.\n  - Anhedonia: Inability to experience positive emotions such as happiness, satisfaction, love, or pleasure.\n  - Indifference to Achievement: Insensitivity or indifference to personal achievements or success.\n  - Feelings of Worthlessness: The feeling that one is useless and has no value.\n  - Ruminative Guilt: A thought pattern of obsessively replaying past minor mistakes or flaws and constantly reproaching oneself.\n  - Excessive or Inappropriate Guilt: Feeling excessive responsibility for trivial matters or daily events and believing one deserves punishment.\n  - Delusional Guilt: Guilt reaching a delusional level, disconnected from reality, involving feeling responsible for unrelated unfortunate events.\n  - Reduced Concentration: Unable to pay attention or maintain focus on specific activities like conversation, reading, or watching TV.\n  - Indecisiveness: Extreme difficulty making even minor daily choices (e.g., menu, clothes) and hesitating to make decisions.\n  - Subjective Memory Complaints: Complaining of memory decline, such as forgetting where things were placed, due to reduced focus.\n  - Clouded Thinking: Thinking feels foggy, dazed, or not clear.\n  Duration: 5 months\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

I've also started pulling away from my friends and family, and I just don't have the energy to put any effort into maintaining those relationships anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience feelings of guilt, worthlessness, or excessive self-blame, and if so, how often do you have these feelings?\n\nPatient: Yes, I constantly feel worthless and useless, and I've been burdened by this intense guilt almost every day for the last five months. I find myself obsessively replaying minor mistakes from my past and constantly blaming myself for trivial daily events. It feels like a non-stop loop in my head that I just can't escape.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about these obsessive thoughts of guilt and self-blame, such as how they started, how long they last, and if you feel compelled to perform any specific rituals or behaviors to try to alleviate them?\n\nPatient: They started about five months ago when this depression set in, and I constantly replay minor past mistakes, feeling like I deserve to be punished. I don't perform any physical rituals or behaviors to try to stop them; instead, I just get stuck in a loop of blaming myself in my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the obsessive thoughts of guilt and self-blame are a primary part of your distress, or do you feel they are more a consequence of your overall feeling of being down and unmotivated?\n\nPatient: Honestly, I'm not sure which one is the cause and which is the effect. The constant self-blame and my lack of motivation feel so deeply interconnected and equally overwhelming that it's hard to separate them.\n\nDoctor: Do you experience any significant anxiety, fear, or apprehension, either in general or in response to specific situations or thoughts, and if so, how do you think it relates to your current state?\n\nPatient: I don't really feel anxious or fearful in general. Instead, I just feel very down, unmotivated, and completely empty inside.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything else you think might be important for me to know about your symptoms or experiences that we haven't discussed yet?\n\nPatient: Lately, my mind just feels incredibly foggy and clouded, and I've been having a really hard time concentrating on basic tasks or making simple daily decisions. I also constantly feel worthless and blame myself for everything, which has been going on for the whole five months. It is just so exhausting to even try to think."
  }
]
